# Popcorn

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="popcorn-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="popcorn-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="popcorn-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Popcorn in the HMI library has 83 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Mn, Zn, V, Se, Ba, Mo, Si and Sr from 10 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 3.8 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 107 µg/kg (Bielecka et al. 2022).<sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2022-poland-gluten-free-cereals-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Markiewicz-Żukowska R, Puścion-Jakubik A, Grabia M, Nowakowski P, Soroczyńska J, Socha K · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2022-poland-gluten-free-cereals-metals">Gluten-Free Cereals and Pseudocereals as a Potential Source of Exposure to Toxic Elements among Polish Residents</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients 2022, 14, 2342</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2022-poland-gluten-free-cereals-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the popcorn came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-popcorn" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-popcorn-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of popcorn, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

original statistic, material, basis and censoring. Each source drawer identifies methods and overlapping samples. No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:popcorn-aae353d1ed32:e07b7128cfe5"><summary>HMI popcorn Pb reference · Popcorn, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:popcorn-aae353d1ed32:e07b7128cfe5"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI popcorn Pb reference · Popcorn, as sold. Popcorn, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤5</td><td>6.5–23 (bounded)</td><td>107</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤5</td><td>6.5–20 (bounded)</td><td>107</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>32 reference units; 3 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>28</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>Bielecka et al. 2022</td><td>1</td><td>33%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for popcorn, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>3 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li><li><a href="../sources/bielecka2022-poland-gluten-free-cereals-metals" class="internal" data-slug="sources/bielecka2022-poland-gluten-free-cereals-metals" data-no-cite-meta>Bielecka et al. 2022</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>123</td><td>123</td><td>123</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>123</td><td>123</td><td>123</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>1 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Ventura et al. 2025</td><td>1</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for popcorn, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>Ventura et al. 2025 is the only compatible sampling family for popcorn, as sold on this basis in the completed popcorn ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/ventura2025-portuguese-total-diet-study-trace-elements" class="internal" data-slug="sources/ventura2025-portuguese-total-diet-study-trace-elements" data-no-cite-meta>Ventura et al. 2025</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:popcorn-0369bed4d5ba:ecb1ff2ac2cd</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:popcorn-90846540215e:76afdad76833"><summary>HMI popcorn Sr reference · Popcorn, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:popcorn-90846540215e:76afdad76833"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI popcorn Sr reference · Popcorn, as sold. ## Mitigation for popcorn

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 4,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Popcorn FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-popcorn" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-popcorn" data-question="What heavy metals are found in popcorn?">

class="cite-card-authors">Elsheikh MA, Ali DMH, Alotaibi SH · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/elsheikh2020-toxic-trace-elements-children-foods-infant-formulae-saudi">Evaluation 32(6):1273-1278</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/elsheikh2020-toxic-trace-elements-children-foods-infant-formulae-saudi">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in popcorn, as sold from 65 results in 3 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 3.4 to 50 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-popcorn" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-popcorn" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in popcorn?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in popcorn?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, popcorn, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (3.4 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (50 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in EURO (4.5 µg/kg) and the highest in EMRO (14 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-popcorn-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-popcorn-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in popcorn is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in popcorn is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 1 result from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in popcorn, as sold from 4 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 15.8 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
